<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> The singular set of every stationary integral <i>m</i>-varifold in an open Euclidean set has Hausdorff dimension at most $`m-1`$, in every positive dimension and codimension. This sharp bound proves the Euclidean singular-set conjecture recorded by Brena, Decio, and De Lellis.

- **claim-singular-set-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every stationary integral $m$-varifold $V$ in an open set $U\subset\mathbb R^{m+n}$, where $m,n\ge1$, $$\dim_{\mathcal H}\operatorname{Sing}V\le m-1.$$
  - Explanation: The singular set of any stationary integral varifold in Euclidean space has Hausdorff dimension at most one less than the varifold dimension, with no minimizing, stability or codimension hypothesis.
- **claim-flat-scales-center-measure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-flat-scales)): Suppose that $V$ is a stationary integral $m$-varifold in an open subset of $\mathbb R^{m+n}$ and that $$m-1<s<\dim_{\mathcal H}\operatorname{Sing}(V).$$ After a translation there exist a fixed point $0\in\operatorname{Sing}(V)$, an integer $Q\ge1$, an $m$-plane $\Pi$ through zero, radii $L_i\downarrow0$, and positive Radon measures $\sigma_i$ such that $$\label{eq:flat-targets} \Theta_V(0)=Q,\qquad V_{L_i}\rightharpoonup Q\|\Pi\| \quad\hbox{locally in }\mathbb R^{m+n}.$$ Each $\sigma_i$ is supported in $\overline{\mathbf B}_1$ on singular points of $V_{L_i}$ having density exactly $Q$. There are constants $c,C>0$, independent of $i$, for which $$\label{eq:center-measure-bounds} \sigma_i(\mathbf B_u(X))\le C u^s \quad(X\in\mathbb R^{m+n},\ u>0),\qquad c\le\sigma_i(\overline{\mathbf B}_1)\le C.$$
  - Explanation: If the singular set is larger than the claimed bound, one finds a singular point with a planar tangent of integer multiplicity and, at the same scales, a nonzero measure of nearby singular points of the same density with a uniform ball-growth bound.
- **claim-scalar-mass-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-input)): Fix dimensions, a bound for $Q$, bounded chart geometry, a mass bound, and an interior enlargement factor $c>1$. Suppose $$s^{-m}\pi_\#\mu(B_{cs})\le C_0, \qquad s^{-m}(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs})\le\epsilon$$ with $\epsilon$ sufficiently small. Put $b=(1+c)/2$. On the intermediate ball $B_{bs}$, the count selects an integer $q\ge0$ with $\operatorname{vol}_S\{y\in B_{bs}:N(y)\ne q\}\le C\epsilon s^m$. There is a fixed finite test order $J_{\mathrm{sc}}$ such that, for every smooth scalar test $\eta$ supported in $B_{bs}$, $$\label{eq:scalar-distribution-before-count} \begin{aligned} \left\|\int\eta\,\mathrm d(\pi_\#\mu-q\operatorname{vol}_S)\right\| &\le C\epsilon s^m\\|\eta\\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s},\\ \\|\eta\\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s} &=\max_{0\le j\le J_{\mathrm{sc}}}s^j\\|D^j\eta\\|_\infty. \end{aligned}$$ The derivatives are in base coordinates; this test order is included among the finite orders fixed for the argument. For every nonnegative smooth test $\chi$ supported in $B_{bs}$, with reference integral one and $s^m\\|\chi\\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s}\le C_1$ for a fixed constant $C_1$, one also has $$\label{eq:scalar-average-before-count} \left\|\int\chi\,\mathrm d(\pi_\#\mu)-q\right\|\le C\epsilon.$$ Here the constant may depend on $C_1$; the factor $s^m$ accounts for the size of a test whose integral, rather than its supremum, is one. In particular, if such an average is within $1/4$ of $Q$, then $q=Q$, and $$\label{eq:scalar-input} \|M\|(B_s)+\operatorname{vol}_S\{y\in B_s:N(y)\ne Q\} \le C(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs}).$$ The same integer propagates along comparable working balls $B_s$ satisfying these bounds, provided their overlaps have fixed positive relative volume. The smallness threshold depends on that overlap fraction.
  - Explanation: Imported scalar estimate: smooth projected mass averages select an integer sheet count, and once it equals the subtracted integer the signed mass excess is controlled by tilt and curvature errors.
- **claim-signed-excess-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-signed-input)): Fix an integer $Q\ge1$ and $\kappa>0$. In the normal-cylinder setting above, scale a physical base radius $l$ to one and suppose $B_4$ is available. Set $\tau=\|M\|+D+\Lambda$. If $$\label{eq:signed-hypotheses} \tau(B_4)\le d=e^{-T},\qquad l+\sup\|z_{\mathrm{phys}}\|\le d^\kappa,$$ where the supremum is over the restricted support above the available chart, then, for $T$ sufficiently large, $$\label{eq:signed-input} \int w\,\mathrm d(2M-D-C_Q\Lambda) \le d\exp[-F_Q(T)]$$ whenever $w$ is supported in $B_1$, $0\le w\le1$, and $\operatorname{Lip}w\le1$. The finite derivative order $J$, threshold, and constants depend only on the stated fixed data. For a subchart in inherited frames, the second condition is imposed on $\gamma$ in Equation <a href="#eq:physical-gamma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-gamma">[eq:physical-gamma]</a>. For a flat reference, take $\Lambda=0$ and omit the second condition.
  - Explanation: Imported signed-excess estimate: a one-sided combination of the mass excess, tilt and curvature error is bounded by a very small decaying quantity when the total budget is small.
- **claim-local-fitting-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-fits)): For every $x\in B_5$, there are smooth minimal graphs $S_{x,\ell}=\operatorname{graph}f_{x,\ell}$ over $B_{80\ell}(x)$, at descending dyadic scales $\ell=2^{-j}$ starting at $j=0$, through the first scale at which $$\sup\{\|X_v-f_{x,\ell}(X_h)\|: X\in\operatorname{spt}\mu,\ X_h\in B_{64\ell}(x)\} \le m_0\ell^k$$ fails. Every constructed fit, including the first failed one, has support height at most $Cm_0\ell^k$ on the fixed interior enlargements of this test ball. On $B_{70\ell}(x)$ its normalized tilt and projected mass defect are bounded by $Cm_0^2\ell^{2k-2}$. For a fixed-base smooth vector test $\eta$ supported in $B_{70\ell}(x)$, put $\eta_{x,\ell}(w)=\eta(x+\ell w)$ on $B_{70}$. Its first height moment is bounded by $Cm_0^2\ell^{m+2k-1}\\|\eta_{x,\ell}\\|_{H^q(B_{70})}$, for some fixed integer $q$. Thus the test norm is taken after rescaling the base ball by $\ell^{-1}$, with no additional amplitude factor. Comparable overlapping fits differ in derivative order $j\le J+1$ by at most $Cm_0^2\ell^{2k-1-j}$ on smaller common balls. Their fixed-coordinate derivatives are uniformly bounded, and their slopes are arbitrarily small when $m_0$ is small.* *At a first failure $(x,\ell)$, every fixed sufficiently small comparable base subball with center in $B_{30\ell}(x)$ contains a set of base volume at least $c\ell^m$ whose normal fibers contain two entries separated by at least $cm_0\ell^k$. Both entries have small tangent tilt relative to the fit and lie at height at most $Cm_0\ell^k$ from it. The constants may depend on the fixed relative subball radius and tilt threshold, but not on the start or the failed scale.
  - Explanation: Local minimal graph fits exist at dyadic scales until a height test fails, with quantitative height, tilt, overlap and first-moment bounds, and a separation property at the first failure.
- **claim-weighted-height-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-bounds)): On <a href="#eq:fitting-radius-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fitting-radius-range">[eq:fitting-radius-range]</a>, $\delta>0$, $\delta\le Cr^{k-1}$, and normalized mass is bounded. For $J_0=m+p+4$ as fixed in Section <a href="#sec:normal-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:normal-inputs">3</a>, $$\begin{aligned} c\delta^{J_0}&\le H/r^2\le C\delta^2, \label{eq:height-power-bounds}\\ \|\mathcal H_S\|+\|\nabla^\perp\mathcal H_S\| &\le C\delta r\quad(\rho<r),\label{eq:point-curvature-bound}\\ \int\phi\bigl(\|\mathcal H_S\|^2+ \|\nabla^\perp\mathcal H_S\|^2\bigr)\,\mathrm d\mu &\le C\int\phi\|z\|^2\,\mathrm d\mu.\label{eq:weighted-curvature-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover a nonnegative smooth test $\chi_r$, with reference integral one and support in $\{\rho<r/4\}$, can be chosen so that its projected mass average is within $1/8$ of $Q$ and $r^m\\|\chi_r\\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_r}\le C$. All constants are uniform among good starts.
  - Explanation: On the fitting radius range the weighted height is positive and bounded above and below by powers of the normalized sup height, and the center curvature quantities are controlled by it.
- **claim-interval-frequency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-interval-frequency)): After fixing $r_*$ sufficiently small, on <a href="#eq:fitting-radius-range" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fitting-radius-range">[eq:fitting-radius-range]</a> the following inequalities hold: $$\begin{aligned} \|P\|&\le C(n_*+r^2)+\eta q_* ,\label{eq:freq-absolute}\\ 2P&\le n_*+Cg(n_*+1)+Cr^2+\eta q_* ,\label{eq:freq-signed}\\ n_*&\le\sqrt{q_*}+Cr^2+\eta n_* ,\label{eq:freq-flux}\\ \left\|\dot A/H-(2P-n_*+q_*)\right\| &\le C\{r\sqrt{q_*}+r^2(1+n_*)\}+\eta(n_*+q_*), \label{eq:freq-horizontal}\\ -m\le\dot H/H&\le2n_*+Cr+\eta(n_*+q_*). \label{eq:freq-height} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\eta=C\delta^\alpha\le C'e^{-cT}$ for fixed positive $\alpha,c$, and constants are uniform among intervals. The functions whose derivatives occur are locally absolutely continuous in $\log r$.
  - Explanation: Quantitative inequalities for the frequency ingredients (signed mass, tilt flux, horizontal and height derivatives) on a fitting interval, with errors that are small in the height scale.
- **claim-inward-control** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inward-control)): There is a fixed large $K$ such that, writing $v_0=\dot A/H$, $$\label{eq:large-frequency} \|P\|\ge K\quad\Longrightarrow\quad q_*/\|P\|\ge c\min(\|P\|,\eta^{-1}),\qquad v_0\ge cq_*.$$ When $K\le P\le T^4$, $$\label{eq:moderate-frequency} \dot P\ge-C(rP+gP^2).$$ A lower bound for $P$ at the smaller-radius endpoint of a finite interval gives a lower bound throughout that interval. On an interval extending to zero, $P$ is bounded below for all sufficiently small radii. If at a sufficiently small starting radius $r_0$ one has $P_0\le T_0^2$, then inward throughout the interval $$\label{eq:inward-upper} P\le \exp(1)\max(K,P_0).$$ Such a starting radius exists on every interval extending to zero. Finally, wherever $\|P\|\le C_0$ and $\eta$ is sufficiently small depending on $C_0$, every fixed bounded-ratio subinterval satisfies $$\label{eq:height-doubling} H(r')\asymp H(r''),\qquad \int q_*\,\mathrm d\log r\le C.$$ The constants in the last conclusion depend on $C_0$ and the radius ratio, not on the position of the interval.
  - Explanation: Differential inequalities for the frequency give inward lower and upper bounds and height doubling on bounded-ratio subintervals.
- **claim-target-frequency-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-target-frequency)): All sufficiently late $L_i$ belong to intervals <a href="#eq:target-interval" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:target-interval">[eq:target-interval]</a>. After passing to a subsequence, either one interval extends to zero and contains all these targets, or the indices of their covering intervals tend to infinity. In the latter case, on each sufficiently late covering interval, $$\label{eq:uniform-frequency-range} \|P(r)\|\le C\qquad(B_*b\le r\le r_*),$$ and <a href="#eq:height-doubling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:height-doubling">[eq:height-doubling]</a> holds there with uniform constants. In the former case the same conclusions hold on the sufficiently small-radius part of the fixed interval.
  - Explanation: All late target scales lie in fitting intervals on which the frequency stays bounded and height doubles uniformly.
- **claim-centered-blowup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-centered-blowup)): Let the stationary integral varifold, singular origin, integer $Q$, plane $\Pi$, and sequence $L_i\downarrow0$ be as in Proposition <a href="#prop:flat-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:flat-scales">2</a>. After taking a subsequence and identifying $\Pi$ with the horizontal plane, there are positive $a_i\to0$ and $C^J$ graph disks $S_i$ over a fixed enlargement of $B_8\subset\Pi$, converging to $\Pi$ through all derivative orders needed below, with the following properties. In target units let $\mu_i$ be the weight of $V_{L_i}$, let $\pi_i$ be nearest projection to $S_i$, and set $$z_i=X-\pi_iX,\qquad M_i=(\pi_i)_\#\mu_i-Q\operatorname{vol}_{S_i},\qquad D_i=(\pi_i)_\#(e_i\mu_i).$$ All measures are restricted to one fixed interior normal cylinder containing the support in question, with a support-free gap at its vertical boundary. On the part over $B_8$, $$\label{eq:centered-interface} \begin{gathered} D_i+\|M_i\|=O(a_i^2)\quad\hbox{in total mass}, \qquad \|z_i\|\le Ca_i\quad\hbox{on the support},\\ \sup\bigl(\|\mathcal H_{S_i}\|+ \|\nabla^\perp\mathcal H_{S_i}\|\bigr)=o(a_i), \qquad a_i^{-1}(\pi_i)_\#(z_i\mu_i)\rightharpoonup0. \end{gathered}$$ Moreover the normalized height-square measures have uniformly bounded mass there and are quantitatively nonzero on a fixed compact subset of $B_4$; in particular, for all sufficiently large $i$, $$\label{eq:nonzero-second-moment} c\le a_i^{-2}\int_{\pi_iX\in B_3}\|z_i\|^2\,\mathrm d\mu_i \le a_i^{-2}\int_{\pi_iX\in B_8}\|z_i\|^2\,\mathrm d\mu_i\le C.$$ Balls in this statement are understood through the graph coordinates over $\Pi$. The weak convergence in <a href="#eq:centered-interface" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:centered-interface">[eq:centered-interface]</a> holds against compactly supported tests in $B_8$.
  - Explanation: At the target scales there are reference graphs and height normalizations with small tilt and mass excess, negligible curvature at the height scale, vanishing first height moment and a nonzero second moment.
- **claim-limiting-measure-equations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-linearized)): Let $V_i$ be stationary integral $m$-varifolds converging locally to $Q\|\Pi\|$, where $Q$ is a positive integer and $\Pi$ is an $m$-plane. Let $S_i$ be reference graphs converging to $\Pi$ in every fixed finite derivative order used below, on a normal cylinder with fixed interior room over a connected disk $\Omega\subset\Pi$. The cylinders contain all support used below and have a support-free gap at their artificial vertical boundaries above each compact base subset. Let $\mu_i$ denote the weight of $V_i$ restricted to this cylinder, $\pi_i$ nearest projection onto $S_i$, and $z_i=X-\pi_iX$. All pushforwards below use this restriction. Use the horizontal and vertical tangent blocks relative to $S_i$ to set $B_i=I-(P_T)_{hh}$, $e_i=\operatorname{tr}B_i$, $D_i=(\pi_i)_\#(e_i\mu_i)$, and $M_i=(\pi_i)_\#\mu_i-Q\operatorname{vol}_{S_i}$. Suppose $a_i\downarrow0$ and, on each compact subdisk with interior room, $$\begin{gathered} \|M_i\|+D_i=O(a_i^2),\qquad \|z_i\|\le Ca_i\quad\hbox{on the support},\\ \|\mathcal H_{S_i}\|+\|\nabla^\perp\mathcal H_{S_i}\|=o(a_i), \qquad a_i^{-1}(\pi_i)_\#(z_i\mu_i)\rightharpoonup0. \end{gathered}$$ Assume also that $a_i^{-2}(\pi_i)_\#(\|z_i\|^2\mu_i)$ has a positive uniform lower mass bound on a fixed compact subset of $\Omega$. Identify the bases by their converging graph coordinates. After passage to a subsequence there are Radon measures satisfying $$\label{eq:limit-measures} \begin{aligned} a_i^{-2}(\pi_i)_\#(\|z_i\|^2\mu_i)&\rightharpoonup\nu,& a_i^{-2}(\pi_i)_\#(B_i\mu_i)&\rightharpoonup\beta,\\ a_i^{-2}D_i&\rightharpoonup d_0=\operatorname{tr}\beta,& a_i^{-2}M_i&\rightharpoonup\mathfrak m,\\ a_i^{-2}(\pi_i)_\#\bigl(z_i\cdot(P_T)_{vh}\,\mu_i\bigr) &\rightharpoonup f. \end{aligned}$$ Here $\nu\ne0$ and $d_0$ are nonnegative measures, $f$ is vector-valued, $\beta$ is symmetric matrix-valued, and $\mathfrak m$ is signed. They obey $$\label{eq:limit-psd} \begin{pmatrix}\nu&f^{\mathsf t}\\ f&\beta\end{pmatrix}\ge0$$ as a matrix-valued measure, and $$\label{eq:limit-identities} \nabla\nu=2f,\qquad \operatorname{div}f=d_0,\qquad \int_\Omega\operatorname{div}X\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m =\int_\Omega DX:\mathrm d\beta \quad (X\in C_c^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb R^m)).$$ Finally, locally on $\Omega$, $$\label{eq:limit-sign} 2\mathfrak m\le d_0,\qquad \|\mathfrak m\|\le C d_0.$$ The data supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:centered-blowup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:centered-blowup">10</a> satisfy these hypotheses, with a fixed disk containing the closed unit ball of density centers.
  - Explanation: The normalized height, tilt, flux and mass-excess quantities converge to measures obeying linear identities, a block positivity condition and a one-sided mass-versus-tilt inequality.
- **claim-density-center-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-density-comparison)): In the setting of Proposition <a href="#prop:linearized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:linearized">11</a>, suppose that $\Omega$ contains a fixed neighborhood of $\overline B_1\subset\Pi$. Let $\sigma_i$ be positive measures supported on density-$Q$ points of $V_i$ in the ambient closed unit ball, with $$c\le\sigma_i(\overline{\mathbf B}_1)\le C, \qquad \sigma_i(\mathbf B_r(X))\le Cr^s \quad(X\in\mathbb R^{m+n},\ r>0),\qquad s>m-1.$$ Pass to a weak limit $\sigma$ along the subsequence defining the linearized measures. Then $\sigma\ne0$, its support lies in $\overline B_1\subset\Pi$, and it has the same upper ball bound. There is $l_*>0$ such that $$\label{eq:center-comparison} 2l^2\int\phi_{x,l}\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m \ge\int\psi_{x,l}\,\mathrm d\nu \qquad(x\in\operatorname{spt}\sigma, 0<l<l_*).$$
  - Explanation: At every limiting density center a kernel-weighted comparison between mass excess and height holds for all small radii, obtained by averaging a corrected radial monotonicity over the center measure.
- **claim-measure-dimension-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-measure-reduction)): Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^m$ be connected and open. Let $\nu$ be a nonzero nonnegative Radon measure, $f$ an $\mathbb R^m$-valued Radon measure, $\beta$ a symmetric matrix-valued Radon measure, and $\mathfrak m$ a signed Radon measure on $\Omega$. Suppose that $$\label{eq:abstract-psd} \begin{pmatrix}\nu&f^{\rm t}\\ f&\beta\end{pmatrix} \quad\hbox{is a positive semidefinite matrix-valued measure}, \qquad d_0=\operatorname{tr}\beta,$$ and that, in distributions, $$\label{eq:abstract-identities} \nabla\nu=2f,\qquad \operatorname{div}f=d_0,\qquad \int\operatorname{div}X\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m =\int DX:\mathrm d\beta \quad(X\in C_c^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb R^m)).$$ Assume also that for every open $\Omega'\Subset\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\Omega'}$ such that, on $\Omega'$, $$\label{eq:abstract-sign} 2\mathfrak m\le d_0,\qquad \|\mathfrak m\|\le C_{\Omega'}d_0.$$ Let $Z\subset\Omega$ be a Borel set such that for each $x\in Z$, $$\label{eq:abstract-center} 2l^2\int\phi_{x,l}\,\mathrm d\mathfrak m \ge\int\psi_{x,l}\,\mathrm d\nu$$ for every sufficiently small $l>0$. Then $\dim_{\mathcal H}Z\le m-1$.
  - Explanation: For measure data with the stated positivity, identities and sign condition, the set of centers satisfying the kernel comparison has Hausdorff dimension at most one less than the ambient dimension.
- **claim-no-zero-height-region** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-limit-height)): For the nonzero measure $\nu$ on the connected set $\Omega$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:measure-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:measure-reduction">13</a>, equations <a href="#eq:abstract-psd" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:abstract-psd">[eq:abstract-psd]</a>–<a href="#eq:abstract-sign" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:abstract-sign">[eq:abstract-sign]</a> imply $H(l)>0$ whenever $\overline B_l(x)\subset\Omega$ and $l>0$.
  - Explanation: The height quantity is positive at every interior ball, so no open region carries zero height measure.

For a flat reference, take $\\Lambda=0$ and omit the second condition.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-fits",
        "exact_quote": "For every $x\\in B_5$, there are smooth minimal graphs $S_{x,\\ell}=\\operatorname{graph}f_{x,\\ell}$ over $B_{80\\ell}(x)$, at descending dyadic scales $\\ell=2^{-j}$ starting at $j=0$, through the first scale at which $$\\sup\\{|X_v-f_{x,\\ell}(X_h)|:\n          X\\in\\operatorname{spt}\\mu,\\ X_h\\in B_{64\\ell}(x)\\}\n                                  \\le m_0\\ell^k$$ fails. Every constructed fit, including the first failed one, has support height at most $Cm_0\\ell^k$ on the fixed interior enlargements of this test ball. On $B_{70\\ell}(x)$ its normalized tilt and projected mass defect are bounded by $Cm_0^2\\ell^{2k-2}$. For a fixed-base smooth vector test $\\eta$ supported in $B_{70\\ell}(x)$, put $\\eta_{x,\\ell}(w)=\\eta(x+\\ell w)$ on $B_{70}$. Its first height moment is bounded by $Cm_0^2\\ell^{m+2k-1}\\|\\eta_{x,\\ell}\\|_{H^q(B_{70})}$, for some fixed integer $q$. Thus the test norm is taken after rescaling the base ball by $\\ell^{-1}$, with no additional amplitude factor. Comparable overlapping fits differ in derivative order $j\\le J+1$ by at most $Cm_0^2\\ell^{2k-1-j}$ on smaller common balls. Their fixed-coordinate derivatives are uniformly bounded, and their slopes are arbitrarily small when $m_0$ is small.*\n\n*At a first failure $(x,\\ell)$, every fixed sufficiently small comparable base subball with center in $B_{30\\ell}(x)$ contains a set of base volume at least $c\\ell^m$ whose normal fibers contain two entries separated by at least $cm_0\\ell^k$. Both entries have small tangent tilt relative to the fit and lie at height at most $Cm_0\\ell^k$ from it. The constants may depend on the fixed relative subball radius and tilt threshold, but not on the start or the failed scale.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inward-control",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed large $K$ such that, writing $v_0=\\dot A/H$, $$\\label{eq:large-frequency}\n |P|\\ge K\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n q_*/|P|\\ge c\\min(|P|,\\eta^{-1}),\\qquad v_0\\ge cq_*.$$ When $K\\le P\\le T^4$, $$\\label{eq:moderate-frequency}\n \\dot P\\ge-C(rP+gP^2).$$ A lower bound for $P$ at the smaller-radius endpoint of a finite interval gives a lower bound throughout that interval. On an interval extending to zero, $P$ is bounded below for all sufficiently small radii. If at a sufficiently small starting radius $r_0$ one has $P_0\\le T_0^2$, then inward throughout the interval $$\\label{eq:inward-upper}\n P\\le \\exp(1)\\max(K,P_0).$$ Such a starting radius exists on every interval extending to zero. Finally, wherever $|P|\\le C_0$ and $\\eta$ is sufficiently small depending on $C_0$, every fixed bounded-ratio subinterval satisfies $$\\label{eq:height-doubling}\n H(r')\\asymp H(r''),\\qquad\n \\int q_*\\,\\mathrm d\\log r\\le C.$$ The constants in the last conclusion depend on $C_0$ and the radius ratio, not on the position of the interval.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

On an interval extending to zero, $P$ is bounded below for all sufficiently small radii. Finally, wherever $|P|\\le C_0$ and $\\eta$ is sufficiently small depending on $C_0$, every fixed bounded-ratio subinterval satisfies $$\\label{eq:height-doubling}\n H(r')\\asymp H(r''),\\qquad\n \\int q_*\\,\\mathrm d\\log r\\le C.$$ The constants in the last conclusion depend on $C_0$ and the radius ratio, not on the position of the interval.",

        "exact_quote": "All sufficiently late $L_i$ belong to intervals <a href=\"#eq:target-interval\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:target-interval\">[eq:target-interval]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-centered-blowup",
        "exact_quote": "Let the stationary integral varifold, singular origin, integer $Q$, plane $\\Pi$, and sequence $L_i\\downarrow0$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:flat-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:flat-scales\">2</a>. After taking a subsequence and identifying $\\Pi$ with the horizontal plane, there are positive $a_i\\to0$ and $C^J$ graph disks $S_i$ over a fixed enlargement of $B_8\\subset\\Pi$, converging to $\\Pi$ through all derivative orders needed below, with the following properties. In target units let $\\mu_i$ be the weight of $V_{L_i}$, let $\\pi_i$ be nearest projection to $S_i$, and set $$z_i=X-\\pi_iX,\\qquad\n M_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#\\mu_i-Q\\operatorname{vol}_{S_i},\\qquad\n D_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#(e_i\\mu_i).$$ All measures are restricted to one fixed interior normal cylinder containing the support in question, with a support-free gap at its vertical boundary. On the part over $B_8$, $$\\label{eq:centered-interface}\n \\begin{gathered}\n D_i+|M_i|=O(a_i^2)\\quad\\hbox{in total mass},\n \\qquad |z_i|\\le Ca_i\\quad\\hbox{on the support},\\\\\n \\sup\\bigl(|\\mathcal H_{S_i}|+\n                  |\\nabla^\\perp\\mathcal H_{S_i}|\\bigr)=o(a_i),\n \\qquad\n a_i^{-1}(\\pi_i)_\\#(z_i\\mu_i)\\rightharpoonup0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Moreover the normalized height-square measures have uniformly bounded mass there and are quantitatively nonzero on a fixed compact subset of $B_4$; in particular, for all sufficiently large $i$, $$\\label{eq:nonzero-second-moment}\n c\\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_3}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\n \\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_8}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\\le C.$$ Balls in this statement are understood through the graph coordinates over $\\Pi$. The weak convergence in <a href=\"#eq:centered-interface\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centered-interface\">[eq:centered-interface]</a> holds against compactly supported tests in $B_8$.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-centered-blowup",
          "exact_quote": "Let the stationary integral varifold, singular origin, integer $Q$, plane $\\Pi$, and sequence $L_i\\downarrow0$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:flat-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:flat-scales\">2</a>. After taking a subsequence and identifying $\\Pi$ with the horizontal plane, there are positive $a_i\\to0$ and $C^J$ graph disks $S_i$ over a fixed enlargement of $B_8\\subset\\Pi$, converging to $\\Pi$ through all derivative orders needed below, with the following properties. In target units let $\\mu_i$ be the weight of $V_{L_i}$, let $\\pi_i$ be nearest projection to $S_i$, and set $$z_i=X-\\pi_iX,\\qquad\n M_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#\\mu_i-Q\\operatorname{vol}_{S_i},\\qquad\n D_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#(e_i\\mu_i).$$ All measures are restricted to one fixed interior normal cylinder containing the support in question, with a support-free gap at its vertical boundary. On the part over $B_8$, $$\\label{eq:centered-interface}\n \\begin{gathered}\n D_i+|M_i|=O(a_i^2)\\quad\\hbox{in total mass},\n \\qquad |z_i|\\le Ca_i\\quad\\hbox{on the support},\\\\\n \\sup\\bigl(|\\mathcal H_{S_i}|+\n                  |\\nabla^\\perp\\mathcal H_{S_i}|\\bigr)=o(a_i),\n \\qquad\n a_i^{-1}(\\pi_i)_\\#(z_i\\mu_i)\\rightharpoonup0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Moreover the normalized height-square measures have uniformly bounded mass there and are quantitatively nonzero on a fixed compact subset of $B_4$; in particular, for all sufficiently large $i$, $$\\label{eq:nonzero-second-moment}\n c\\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_3}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\n \\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_8}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\\le C.$$ Balls in this statement are understood through the graph coordinates over $\\Pi$. The weak convergence in <a href=\"#eq:centered-interface\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centered-interface\">[eq:centered-interface]</a> holds against compactly supported tests in $B_8$.",
          "source_id": "PM-346.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let the stationary integral varifold, singular origin, integer $Q$, plane $\\Pi$, and sequence $L_i\\downarrow0$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:flat-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:flat-scales\">2</a>. After taking a subsequence and identifying $\\Pi$ with the horizontal plane, there are positive $a_i\\to0$ and $C^J$ graph disks $S_i$ over a fixed enlargement of $B_8\\subset\\Pi$, converging to $\\Pi$ through all derivative orders needed below, with the following properties. In target units let $\\mu_i$ be the weight of $V_{L_i}$, let $\\pi_i$ be nearest projection to $S_i$, and set $$z_i=X-\\pi_iX,\\qquad\n M_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#\\mu_i-Q\\operatorname{vol}_{S_i},\\qquad\n D_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#(e_i\\mu_i).$$ All measures are restricted to one fixed interior normal cylinder containing the support in question, with a support-free gap at its vertical boundary. On the part over $B_8$, $$\\label{eq:centered-interface}\n \\begin{gathered}\n D_i+|M_i|=O(a_i^2)\\quad\\hbox{in total mass},\n \\qquad |z_i|\\le Ca_i\\quad\\hbox{on the support},\\\\\n \\sup\\bigl(|\\mathcal H_{S_i}|+\n                  |\\nabla^\\perp\\mathcal H_{S_i}|\\bigr)=o(a_i),\n \\qquad\n a_i^{-1}(\\pi_i)_\\#(z_i\\mu_i)\\rightharpoonup0.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Moreover the normalized height-square measures have uniformly bounded mass there and are quantitatively nonzero on a fixed compact subset of $B_4$; in particular, for all sufficiently large $i$, $$\\label{eq:nonzero-second-moment}\n c\\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_3}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\n \\le a_i^{-2}\\int_{\\pi_iX\\in B_8}|z_i|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_i\\le C.$$ Balls in this statement are understood through the graph coordinates over $\\Pi$. The weak convergence in <a href=\"#eq:centered-interface\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centered-interface\">[eq:centered-interface]</a> holds against compactly supported tests in $B_8$.",
      "explanation": "At the target scales there are reference graphs and height normalizations with small tilt and mass excess, negligible curvature at the height scale, vanishing first height moment and a nonzero second moment.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-linearized",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_i$ be stationary integral $m$-varifolds converging locally to $Q|\\Pi|$, where $Q$ is a positive integer and $\\Pi$ is an $m$-plane. Let $S_i$ be reference graphs converging to $\\Pi$ in every fixed finite derivative order used below, on a normal cylinder with fixed interior room over a connected disk $\\Omega\\subset\\Pi$. The cylinders contain all support used below and have a support-free gap at their artificial vertical boundaries above each compact base subset. Let $\\mu_i$ denote the weight of $V_i$ restricted to this cylinder, $\\pi_i$ nearest projection onto $S_i$, and $z_i=X-\\pi_iX$. All pushforwards below use this restriction. Use the horizontal and vertical tangent blocks relative to $S_i$ to set $B_i=I-(P_T)_{hh}$, $e_i=\\operatorname{tr}B_i$, $D_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#(e_i\\mu_i)$, and $M_i=(\\pi_i)_\\#\\mu_i-Q\\operatorname{vol}_{S_i}$. Suppose $a_i\\downarrow0$ and, on each compact subdisk with interior room, $$\\begin{gathered}\n |M_i|+D_i=O(a_i^2),\\qquad |z_i|\\le Ca_i\\quad\\hbox{on the support},\\\\\n |\\mathcal H_{S_i}|+|\\nabla^\\perp\\mathcal H_{S_i}|=o(a_i),\n \\qquad a_i^{-1}(\\pi_i)_\\#(z_i\\mu_i)\\rightharpoonup0.\n\\end{gathered}$$ Assume also that $a_i^{-2}(\\pi_i)_\\#(|z_i|^2\\mu_i)$ has a positive uniform lower mass bound on a fixed compact subset of $\\Omega$. Identify the bases by their converging graph coordinates. After passage to a subsequence there are Radon measures satisfying $$\\label{eq:limit-measures}\n\\begin{aligned}\n a_i^{-2}(\\pi_i)_\\#(|z_i|^2\\mu_i)&\\rightharpoonup\\nu,&\n a_i^{-2}(\\pi_i)_\\#(B_i\\mu_i)&\\rightharpoonup\\beta,\\\\\n a_i^{-2}D_i&\\rightharpoonup d_0=\\operatorname{tr}\\beta,&\n a_i^{-2}M_i&\\rightharpoonup\\mathfrak m,\\\\\n a_i^{-2}(\\pi_i)_\\#\\bigl(z_i\\cdot(P_T)_{vh}\\,\\mu_i\\bigr)\n   &\\rightharpoonup f.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\nu\\ne0$ and $d_0$ are nonnegative measures, $f$ is vector-valued, $\\beta$ is symmetric matrix-valued, and $\\mathfrak m$ is signed. They obey $$\\label{eq:limit-psd}\n \\begin{pmatrix}\\nu&f^{\\mathsf t}\\\\ f&\\beta\\end{pmatrix}\\ge0$$ as a matrix-valued measure, and $$\\label{eq:limit-identities}\n \\nabla\\nu=2f,\\qquad \\operatorname{div}f=d_0,\\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega\\operatorname{div}X\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n   =\\int_\\Omega DX:\\mathrm d\\beta\n \\quad (X\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega;\\mathbb R^m)).$$ Finally, locally on $\\Omega$, $$\\label{eq:limit-sign}\n             2\\mathfrak m\\le d_0,\\qquad\n             |\\mathfrak m|\\le C d_0.$$ The data supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:centered-blowup\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:centered-blowup\">10</a> satisfy these hypotheses, with a fixed disk containing the closed unit ball of density centers.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:linearized\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:linearized\">11</a>, suppose that $\\Omega$ contains a fixed neighborhood of $\\overline B_1\\subset\\Pi$. Let $\\sigma_i$ be positive measures supported on density-$Q$ points of $V_i$ in the ambient closed unit ball, with $$c\\le\\sigma_i(\\overline{\\mathbf B}_1)\\le C,\n \\qquad \\sigma_i(\\mathbf B_r(X))\\le Cr^s\n \\quad(X\\in\\mathbb R^{m+n},\\ r>0),\\qquad s>m-1.$$ Pass to a weak limit $\\sigma$ along the subsequence defining the linearized measures. Then $\\sigma\\ne0$, its support lies in $\\overline B_1\\subset\\Pi$, and it has the same upper ball bound. There is $l_*>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:center-comparison}\n 2l^2\\int\\phi_{x,l}\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n       \\ge\\int\\psi_{x,l}\\,\\mathrm d\\nu\n \\qquad(x\\in\\operatorname{spt}\\sigma, 0<l<l_*).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-346.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:linearized\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:linearized\">11</a>, suppose that $\\Omega$ contains a fixed neighborhood of $\\overline B_1\\subset\\Pi$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-measure-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be connected and open. Let $\\nu$ be a nonzero nonnegative Radon measure, $f$ an $\\mathbb R^m$-valued Radon measure, $\\beta$ a symmetric matrix-valued Radon measure, and $\\mathfrak m$ a signed Radon measure on $\\Omega$. Suppose that $$\\label{eq:abstract-psd}\n \\begin{pmatrix}\\nu&f^{\\rm t}\\\\ f&\\beta\\end{pmatrix}\n \\quad\\hbox{is a positive semidefinite matrix-valued measure},\n \\qquad d_0=\\operatorname{tr}\\beta,$$ and that, in distributions, $$\\label{eq:abstract-identities}\n \\nabla\\nu=2f,\\qquad \\operatorname{div}f=d_0,\\qquad\n \\int\\operatorname{div}X\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n       =\\int DX:\\mathrm d\\beta\n \\quad(X\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega;\\mathbb R^m)).$$ Assume also that for every open $\\Omega'\\Subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\\Omega'}$ such that, on $\\Omega'$, $$\\label{eq:abstract-sign}\n 2\\mathfrak m\\le d_0,\\qquad |\\mathfrak m|\\le C_{\\Omega'}d_0.$$ Let $Z\\subset\\Omega$ be a Borel set such that for each $x\\in Z$, $$\\label{eq:abstract-center}\n 2l^2\\int\\phi_{x,l}\\,\\mathrm d\\mathfrak m\n       \\ge\\int\\psi_{x,l}\\,\\mathrm d\\nu$$ for every sufficiently small $l>0$. Then $\\dim_{\\mathcal H}Z\\le m-1$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-limit-height",
        "exact_quote": "For the nonzero measure $\\nu$ on the connected set $\\Omega$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:measure-reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:measure-reduction\">13</a>, equations <a href=\"#eq:abstract-psd\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:abstract-psd\">[eq:abstract-psd]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:abstract-sign\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:abstract-sign\">[eq:abstract-sign]</a> imply $H(l)>0$ whenever $\\overline B_l(x)\\subset\\Omega$ and $l>0$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **integral varifold weight** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An integral $m$-varifold $V$ has weight $\mu=\theta\mathcal H^m\llcorner E$, where $E$ is countably $m$-rectifiable and $\theta$ is positive, integer-valued, and locally integrable.
- **singular set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We denote the remaining support points by $\operatorname{Sing}V$.
- **good start** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *good start* is a sufficiently small dyadic scale $R$ for which $V_R$ is within a fixed small weak-varifold distance of a multiplicity-$Q$ plane through the origin in $\mathbf B_{2000}$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-flat-scales** [claim](#stmt-prop-flat-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-flat-scales): Frostman-type compact subset, tangent cone with translation invariance, integer density level set -> singular point, planar tangent of integer multiplicity, center measures
- **step-scalar-input** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-input) / [proof](#stmt-lem-scalar-input): imported scalar mass estimate -> integer sheet count and mass-excess control
- **step-signed-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-signed-input) / [proof](#stmt-thm-signed-input): imported signed-excess theorem -> one-sided excess estimate
- **step-local-fits** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-fits) / [proof](#stmt-lem-local-fits): imported local minimal fits and failure separation -> glued reference graph
- **step-weighted-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-bounds): witness argument at failed fits, cell covering -> height and curvature bounds
- **step-interval-frequency** [claim](#stmt-lem-interval-frequency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-interval-frequency): signed excess on bulk cells, radial test on thin collar, flux and height identities -> frequency inequalities
- **step-inward-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-inward-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inward-control): ODE comparison for the frequency -> inward bounds and height doubling
- **step-target-frequency** [claim](#stmt-prop-target-frequency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-target-frequency): restart construction, endpoint bounds from successive failures -> uniform frequency bound at targets
- **step-centered-blowup** [claim](#stmt-prop-centered-blowup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-centered-blowup): height normalization, curvature estimate, mean-value bound, first-moment cell estimate -> centered normalized graphs
- **step-linearized** [claim](#stmt-prop-linearized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linearized): weak compactness, curved first variation, signed excess in rescaled cells -> limiting measure system
- **step-density-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-density-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-density-comparison): geodesic radial test, averaging over centers, fixed-radius expansion -> comparison at density centers
- **step-positive-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-limit-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-limit-height): finite-radius frequency argument by contradiction -> positive height at interior balls
- **step-measure-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-measure-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-limit-height-2): second blow-up at Frostman centers, constant frequency, equality in positive matrix -> dimension bound for centers
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-limit-height-3): contradiction combining all reductions; sharpness by crossing planes -> dimension bound for the singular set

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-singular-dim-bound**: For a stationary integral varifold in an open Euclidean set, the Hausdorff dimension of the singular set never exceeds the varifold dimension minus one, for all dimensions and codimensions.
- **inv-sharpness-crossing-planes**: The codimension-one bound is attained: two distinct multiplicity-one m-planes meeting along an (m-1)-plane form a stationary varifold whose singular set has dimension exactly m-1.
- **inv-abstract-measure-reduction**: For a limiting tuple of height, flux, stress and signed mass measures with the stated positivity and identity constraints, any Borel set of centers satisfying the center comparison for all small radii has Hausdorff dimension at most m-1.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-curved-ambient**: Apply the dimension bound to stationary varifolds in curved ambient metrics. -> The bound is proved only in Euclidean space; curved-metric examples with planar tangents and infinite local topology exist and are not covered.
- **anti-codim-two-bound**: Expect the stronger area-minimizing singular-set dimension bound for general stationary varifolds. -> Crossing sheets give singular sets of dimension exactly m-1, so a smaller dimension bound fails.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result is a non-constructive analytic theorem proved by contradiction through blow-ups, compactness and weak limits of measures on continuum objects; it gives no finite procedure, so no executable reference code or oracle exists. The source states it as a bound on Hausdorff dimension of the singular set of arbitrary stationary integral varifolds.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-euclidean-ambient** (hypothesis): The dimension bound is proved only for stationary integral varifolds in an open Euclidean set; curved ambient metrics are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to stationary varifolds in curved or non-Euclidean ambient metrics']
- **limit-stationarity-integrality** (hypothesis): Vanishing first variation and integer multiplicity are used throughout, since monotonicity, integral compactness and constancy of multiplicity on planar tangents depend on them. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for varifolds with nonzero mean curvature', 'Using the theorem for non-integral varifolds']
- **limit-sharpness-crossing-planes** (edge_case): The bound cannot be lowered for general stationary varifolds: two distinct planes meeting along an (m-1)-plane give a singular set of dimension exactly m-1. Blocked use: ['Expecting a codimension-two singular-set bound for general stationary varifolds']
- **limit-imported-estimates** (formal_scope): The scalar mass estimate, the signed-excess theorem and the local fitting inputs are imported from the companion paper and not proved here, so the argument is conditional on them. Blocked use: ['Treating the argument as self-contained without the companion results']
- **limit-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The proof argues by contradiction using compactness, Egoroff and Lusin type selections and weak limits; it yields no procedure to locate singular points or compute their dimension. Blocked use: ['Reading the proof as an algorithm for finding singular points', 'Using it to certify regularity of a concrete discretized surface']
- **limit-exponent-threshold** (hypothesis): The decisive integral over radii is finite exactly when the exponent exceeds m-1, so the density-center argument works only for exponents above that threshold. Blocked use: ['Running the density-center comparison with an exponent at or below m-1']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


The singular set of every stationary integral <i>m</i>-varifold in an open Euclidean set has Hausdorff dimension at most $`m-1`$, in every positive dimension and codimension. This sharp bound proves the Euclidean singular-set conjecture recorded by Brena, Decio, and De Lellis.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Stationarity is the weakest variational condition naturally imposed on a minimal submanifold: the first variation of area vanishes under every compactly supported ambient deformation. Integral varifolds retain this condition under weak limits while allowing integer multiplicity, self-intersections, and singularities. Their general regularity problem asks how much of the support must nevertheless be a smooth embedded minimal submanifold.

Let $m,n\ge1$ and let $U\subset\mathbb R^{m+n}$ be open. An integral $m$-varifold $V$ has weight $\mu=\theta\mathcal H^m\llcorner E$, where $E$ is countably $m$-rectifiable and $\theta$ is positive, integer-valued, and locally integrable. At $\mu$-almost every $x$, write $T_xV$ for the approximate tangent $m$-plane and $P_{T_xV}$ for its orthogonal projection. Stationarity means <a id="eq:stationarity"></a>$$\label{eq:stationarity}
 \int\operatorname{tr}(P_{T_xV}DX(x))\,\mathrm d\mu(x)=0
 \qquad\text{for every }X\in C_c^1(U;\mathbb R^{m+n}).$$ A point $p\in\operatorname{spt}\mu\cap U$ is *regular* if some neighborhood $W$ of $p$ contains a smooth embedded minimal $m$-submanifold $\Sigma$, without boundary in $W$, such that $\operatorname{spt}\mu\cap W=\Sigma$ and $V\llcorner W=q|\Sigma|$ for a positive integer $q$. We denote the remaining support points by $\operatorname{Sing}V$. Thus regularity includes equality of the local support with one embedded submanifold, not merely a smooth parametrization on a set of full measure. For stationary integral varifolds this is equivalent to local $C^1$ embeddedness of the support, by constancy and elliptic regularity.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every stationary integral $m$-varifold $V$ in an open set $U\subset\mathbb R^{m+n}$, where $m,n\ge1$, $$\dim_{\mathcal H}\operatorname{Sing}V\le m-1.$$*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves the Euclidean conjecture recorded as Conjecture 1.1 by Brena, Decio, and De Lellis (Brena, Decio, and De Lellis 2025) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BDD2025](#ref-BDD2025)]. There is no stability, minimizing, orientability, density-upper-bound, or codimension hypothesis. The estimate is sharp: the sum of the varifolds of two distinct $m$-planes meeting along an $(m-1)$-plane is stationary, and its singular set is exactly that intersection.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and antecedents

Allard’s compactness and regularity theory (Allard 1972) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Allard1972](#ref-Allard1972)] supplies the basic passage from weak convergence to geometric information and gives a relatively open dense regular set. The dimension-reduction method originating in (Federer 1970) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Federer1970](#ref-Federer1970)] leads to the classical stratification by translation symmetries of tangent cones. For stationary integral varifolds, the set of points admitting no planar tangent has Hausdorff dimension at most $m-1$. Naber and Valtorta (Naber and Valtorta 2020) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[NV2020](#ref-NV2020)] proved rectifiability of these classical strata for integral varifolds with bounded mean curvature. The remaining difficulty is therefore the singular set at higher-multiplicity planar tangents, where Allard’s multiplicity-one regularity theorem does not apply. Standard treatments of the compactness, monotonicity, and tangent-cone theory used here include (Federer 1969; Simon 1983) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Federer1969](#ref-Federer1969), [Simon1983](#ref-Simon1983)].

For area-minimizing integral currents, Almgren’s regularity theory (Almgren 2000) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Almgren2000](#ref-Almgren2000)] gives an interior singular-set bound of dimension $m-2$ in arbitrary codimension. De Lellis and Spadaro developed a new proof based on multiple-valued functions, center manifolds, and a frequency analysis of the blow-up (De Lellis and Spadaro 2011; De Lellis and Spadaro 2016a, 2016b) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DLS2011](#ref-DLS2011), [DLS2016II](#ref-DLS2016II), [DLS2016III](#ref-DLS2016III)]. These techniques explain the role of subtracting a well-chosen smooth center before normalizing the height. Their regularity theorems do not apply to arbitrary stationary integral varifolds; even the crossing example above prevents the same dimension bound in our setting.

Hirsch and Spolaor proved the $m-1$ bound for two-valued stationary Lipschitz graphs (Hirsch and Spolaor 2023) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HSgraphs](#ref-HSgraphs)]. Their use of generalized-gradient Young measures retains height and gradient information under weak convergence and is a methodological antecedent of our measure-valued blow-up, which does not assume graphicality or two-valuedness. Hirsch and Spolaor also proved almost-everywhere regularity for stationary codimension-one integral currents whose density is at most two at every support point (Hirsch and Spolaor 2025) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HS2025](#ref-HS2025)]. Krummel, Minter, and Wickramasekera (Krummel, Minter, and Wickramasekera 2026) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[KMW2026](#ref-KMW2026)] obtained singular-set bounds of dimension at most $m-1$ at controlled densities in classes satisfying an explicit epsilon-regularity assumption.

A different line of work addresses higher-order rectifiability. Brakke’s work gives $C^{1,\alpha}$ rectifiability, for every $0<\alpha<1$, of stationary integral varifolds (Brakke 1978) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Brakke1978](#ref-Brakke1978)]. Menne proved second-order rectifiability under locally bounded first variation (Menne 2013) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Menne2013](#ref-Menne2013)]. Brena, De Lellis, and Franceschini (Brena, De Lellis, and Franceschini 2025) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BDLF2025](#ref-BDLF2025)] established smooth rectifiability for stationary integral varifolds; Kolasiński (Kolasiński 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Kolasinski2026](#ref-Kolasinski2026)] subsequently gave an independent alternative proof, following a program he attributes to Menne. Smooth rectifiability describes the measure up to a null set by smooth pieces; it does not assert that the entire support near almost every point equals one such piece. This distinction is essential for the singular set defined above.

The ambient geometry is also important. De Lellis, Hirsch, Lihn, and Spolaor (De Lellis et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DLHLS2026](#ref-DLHLS2026)] construct stationary integral varifolds with planar tangents and infinite local topology in smooth, generally non-Euclidean metrics. Their examples do not contradict the Euclidean dimension bound proved here.

Brena, Decio, and De Lellis (Brena, Decio, and De Lellis 2025, Conjectures 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BDD2025](#ref-BDD2025)] separate the dimension bound from the weaker assertion $\mathcal H^m(\operatorname{Sing}V)=0$. The latter Euclidean assertion is proved in *Almost-everywhere regularity of stationary integral varifolds* (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)], which we henceforth call AE. Our proof uses AE’s signed-excess theorem and local estimates from its fitting and frequency arguments. The elliptic correction of the averaged graph in (Brena, De Lellis, and Franceschini 2025, secs. 6.3–6.4) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BDLF2025](#ref-BDLF2025)] is an antecedent of this fitting construction. The almost-everywhere regularity conclusion itself is not an input: a set can have zero $\mathcal H^m$ measure and still have Hausdorff dimension greater than $m-1$. We state the imported measure estimates in Section <a href="#sec:normal-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:normal-inputs">3</a>. Section <a href="#sec:fitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fitting">4</a> specifies the local fitting estimates and verifies the hypotheses needed for their use at the prescribed scales.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof and its new components

Suppose that $\dim_{\mathcal H}\operatorname{Sing}V>m-1$ and choose an exponent $s$ strictly between these two numbers. A first dimension-reduction argument produces a singular point, placed at the origin, an integer $Q$, and radii $L_i\downarrow0$ for which the rescaled varifolds converge to the multiplicity-$Q$ plane. At these same scales there are nonzero measures $\sigma_i$ carried by density-$Q$ points in a fixed compact ball, with mass bounded above and away from zero and the estimate $$\sigma_i(\mathbf B_r(x))\le Cr^s.$$ Here $\mathbf B_r(x)$ is an ambient Euclidean ball. The limiting measure of centers cannot be carried by an $(m-1)$-plane. This first reduction, proved in Section <a href="#sec:flat-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flat-scales">2</a>, is the familiar geometric starting point of dimension reduction; the challenge is to retain these centers in a further, height-normalized limit.

The proof has four further stages. First, we construct smooth reference graphs at the prescribed scales $L_i$. AE’s minimal fitting gives intervals of radii on which a centered height admits frequency control. Section <a href="#sec:fitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fitting">4</a> proves that these intervals cover every sufficiently small prescribed flat scale, including their transition regions. Section <a href="#sec:centered-blowup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:centered-blowup">5</a> then normalizes the height by numbers $a_i\downarrow0$. The normalized second moment is nonzero, the first moment tends to zero, and the reference graphs’ mean curvature and its first derivative are $o(a_i)$. Both moment conclusions are needed: subtracting a reference graph must neither remove the entire limit nor leave an arbitrary translation in it.

Second, Section <a href="#sec:linearized" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:linearized">6</a> passes the first variations and signed excess to measures on the limiting plane. The resulting data consist of a nonnegative height measure, a positive-semidefinite tilt measure, a mixed height–tilt measure, and a signed mass measure. They satisfy linear first-variation identities, a positive-semidefinite block inequality, and a one-sided comparison of mass and tilt. This formulation avoids assuming that the limiting sheets can be globally labeled or represented by a single-valued function.

Third, the density of the points carrying $\sigma_i$ must still be visible after division by $a_i^2$. Ordinary uncorrected radial comparison does not provide that precision on curved reference graphs. Section <a href="#sec:density-centers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:density-centers">7</a> uses geodesic radial fields weighted by the inverse reference-volume density. The resulting curvature error can be averaged against $\sigma_i$ at every radius down to zero. For a fixed $l>0$, its decisive integral is $$\int_0^l t^{s-m}\,\mathrm dt<\infty,$$ which holds exactly when $s>m-1$. The centered first-moment estimate then removes the translation terms and yields a density comparison at every limiting center.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:dimension-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dimension-reduction">8</a> isolates an abstract dimension-reduction statement for these measures. Its frequency is monotone at the retained centers. A second blow-up makes that frequency constant at enough independent centers. Equality in the positive-semidefinite inequality then forces the height measure to vanish, contrary to its normalization. This is the contradiction that proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

The prescribed-scale construction, the averaged curved radial comparison, and the final measure-valued reduction are the parts needed to strengthen AE’s almost-everywhere conclusion to a dimension bound. The center-manifold and frequency viewpoint belongs to the broader regularity tradition described above; our specific local fitting and signed-excess inputs are those of AE. The initial and final reductions use the classical measure-theoretic framework of (Federer 1969; Mattila 1995) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Federer1969](#ref-Federer1969), [Mattila1995](#ref-Mattila1995)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions

All assertions are local in $U$, and supports are relative to $U$ until a blow-up is taken. If $\eta_r(x)=x/r$, then $V_r=(\eta_r)_\#V$ is the varifold pushforward, whose weight is $r^{-m}(\eta_r)_\#\mu$. Translated blow-ups will be specified explicitly. Bold balls $\mathbf B_r$ are ambient, whereas $B_r$ denotes a base-coordinate ball. The letters $C,c>0$ denote constants depending only on previously fixed data and may change between occurrences. Weak convergence of Radon measures is always tested on continuous compactly supported functions. Positive semidefiniteness of a symmetric matrix-valued measure means that its contraction with every constant vector is a nonnegative measure.

<a id="sec:flat-scales"></a>
### Flat scales carrying many singular centers

The first reduction uses only stationary monotonicity and Hausdorff measure theory. If the singular set has dimension greater than $m-1$, we shall find a singular point with a planar tangent cone and retain, at the same scales, a nonzero measure of nearby points having exactly the same density. Retaining this measure is essential: a planar tangent cone at a single singular point gives no contradiction.

<a id="eq:density-upper-limit"></a>
For $p\in U$ and $r>0$, write $$\eta_{p,r}(X)=\frac{X-p}{r},\qquad
 V_{p,r}=(\eta_{p,r})_\#V.$$ Thus the weight of $V_{p,r}$ is $r^{-m}(\eta_{p,r})_\#\mu$. After translating $p$ to the origin we abbreviate $V_{p,r}$ to $V_r$. We use $\mathbf B_r$ for ambient balls and, later, $B_r$ for balls in an $m$-dimensional base plane. The stationary density is $$\Theta_V(p)=\lim_{r\downarrow0}
       \frac{\mu(\mathbf B_r(p))}{\omega_m r^m}.$$ It exists at every support point, is at least one, and is locally bounded and upper semicontinuous. Moreover, under locally mass-bounded convergence of stationary integral varifolds, $$\label{eq:density-upper-limit}
 V_i\rightharpoonup V_\infty,\quad p_i\longrightarrow p
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \Theta_{V_\infty}(p)\ge\limsup_i\Theta_{V_i}(p_i).$$ These facts follow from monotonicity and integral compactness (Allard 1972; Simon 1983) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Allard1972](#ref-Allard1972), [Simon1983](#ref-Simon1983)]. For example, to obtain <a href="#eq:density-upper-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:density-upper-limit">[eq:density-upper-limit]</a>, bound each density by its mass ratio on a fixed ball, pass to the limit at radii whose boundary has zero limiting mass, and then let the radius decrease to zero.

We shall also use two consequences of these facts. First, supports converge locally under this varifold convergence. The lower density bound prevents support points from persisting where the limit has zero mass; conversely, any neighborhood of a limiting support point has positive limiting mass and therefore meets the approximating supports. Second, $\operatorname{spt}\mu$ has locally finite $\mathcal H^m$ measure. Indeed the lower density bound gives $\mu(\mathbf B_r(p))\ge\omega_m r^m$ whenever the ball is interior and $p\in\operatorname{spt}\mu$. A disjoint-ball covering argument on a compact subset of $U$ bounds its Hausdorff measure by a dimensional constant times the mass in a slightly larger compact set. No local Hausdorff-measure assumption on the support is needed.

<a id="stmt-prop-flat-scales"></a>
<div id="prop:flat-scales" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:center-measure-bounds"></a>
**Proposition 2** (Flat scales and a measure of centers). *Suppose that $V$ is a stationary integral $m$-varifold in an open subset of $\mathbb R^{m+n}$ and that $$m-1<s<\dim_{\mathcal H}\operatorname{Sing}(V).$$ After a translation there exist a fixed point $0\in\operatorname{Sing}(V)$, an integer $Q\ge1$, an $m$-plane $\Pi$ through zero, radii $L_i\downarrow0$, and positive Radon measures $\sigma_i$ such that <a id="eq:flat-targets"></a>$$\label{eq:flat-targets}
 \Theta_V(0)=Q,\qquad V_{L_i}\rightharpoonup Q|\Pi|
 \quad\hbox{locally in }\mathbb R^{m+n}.$$ Each $\sigma_i$ is supported in $\overline{\mathbf B}_1$ on singular points of $V_{L_i}$ having density exactly $Q$. There are constants $c,C>0$, independent of $i$, for which $$\label{eq:center-measure-bounds}
 \sigma_i(\mathbf B_u(X))\le C u^s
 \quad(X\in\mathbb R^{m+n},\ u>0),\qquad
 c\le\sigma_i(\overline{\mathbf B}_1)\le C.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-flat-scales"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-flat-scales</summary>

<a id="eq:initial-frostman"></a>
*Proof.* The regular set is relatively open in $\operatorname{spt}\mu$, so the singular set is Borel. The compact-subset theorem for Hausdorff measure (Mattila 1995, Theorem 8.13) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Mattila1995](#ref-Mattila1995)] gives a compact subset of $\operatorname{Sing}(V)$ with finite positive $\mathcal H^s$ measure. We recall the two measure-theoretic refinements that we need (Federer 1969; Mattila 1995) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Federer1969](#ref-Federer1969), [Mattila1995](#ref-Mattila1995)]. On a set of finite positive $\mathcal H^s$ measure, the upper $s$-density is positive and finite almost everywhere. By restricting to a positive-measure subset where its upper bound is uniform, and then taking a compact subset, we obtain a compact set $K$ such that $$\label{eq:initial-frostman}
 0<\mathcal H^s(K)<\infty,\qquad
 \mathcal H^s(K\cap\mathbf B_r(X))\le C r^s.$$ An upper bound initially obtained for centers in $K$ extends to arbitrary centers by doubling the radius. Enlarging $C$ handles the remaining large radii. Lusin’s theorem permits a further compact restriction of positive measure on which $\Theta_V$ is continuous. The upper bound in <a href="#eq:initial-frostman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-frostman">[eq:initial-frostman]</a> is preserved.

For $\mathcal H^s$-almost every $p\in K$, positive upper density supplies radii $r_i\downarrow0$ and a constant $c_p>0$ with $$\mathcal H^s(K\cap\mathbf B_{r_i}(p))\ge c_p r_i^s.$$ Pass to a subsequence on which $V_{p,r_i}$ converges to a stationary integral tangent cone $C_p$. Define measures on the closed unit ball by $$\lambda_i=r_i^{-s}(\eta_{p,r_i})_\#
       \big(\mathcal H^s\!\llcorner(K\cap\overline{\mathbf B}_{r_i}(p))\big).$$ They have uniformly bounded mass, mass bounded away from zero, and the upper ball bound in <a href="#eq:initial-frostman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-frostman">[eq:initial-frostman]</a>. A further subsequence converges to a nonzero measure $\lambda$ with the same upper ball bound. Its support lies in $\operatorname{spt}C_p$.

Set $q_p=\Theta_V(p)$; at this point $q_p$ is not assumed to be an integer. If $z\in\operatorname{spt}\lambda$, there are points $p_i\in K$ with $(p_i-p)/r_i\to z$. Continuity on $K$ and <a href="#eq:density-upper-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:density-upper-limit">[eq:density-upper-limit]</a> imply $$\Theta_{C_p}(z)\ge q_p.$$ On the other hand, the density at infinity of $C_p$ about $z$ equals $q_p$: sandwich $\mathbf B_R(z)$ between the balls about zero of radii $R-|z|$ and $R+|z|$, and use conicality about zero. Monotonicity about $z$ consequently has equality at every radius.

We spell out the geometric consequence of this equality. The radial displacement $X-z$ lies in the tangent plane of $C_p$ almost everywhere. Testing stationarity with $\varphi(X)(X-z)$ gives $$\int\big(m\varphi+(X-z)\cdot\nabla\varphi\big)
       \,\mathrm d\|C_p\|=0.$$ Differentiating dilated scalar integrals shows that the weight is $m$-homogeneous about $z$. Since a rectifiable weight determines its tangent plane almost everywhere, the varifold is conical about $z$ as well. Composing dilations about $z$ and about zero now gives translation invariance along the whole line $\mathbb Rz$.

The support of $\lambda$ spans at least $m$ linearly independent directions. Otherwise it lies in a linear space of dimension at most $m-1$; covering its bounded support by $O(\varepsilon^{-(m-1)})$ balls of radius $\varepsilon$ would give $\lambda(\mathbb R^{m+n})\le C\varepsilon^{s-m+1}\to0$. Choose $m$ independent such directions and let $P$ be their span. The cone is invariant under every translation in $P$. If its support contained a point outside $P$, subtracting its $P$-component would give $w\in P^\perp\setminus\{0\}$ in the support. Conicality and translation invariance would then place every disk $tw+(P\cap\mathbf B_1)$, $1\le t\le2$, in the support. These are disjoint disks of equal positive $m$-dimensional measure, all in a bounded region, contradicting local finiteness of $\mathcal H^m(\operatorname{spt}C_p)$. Thus its support is precisely $P$. Tangential stationarity on this plane gives constant multiplicity, and integrality gives $$C_p=q_p|P|,\qquad q_p\in\{1,2,\ldots\}.$$

We have proved that $\Theta_V$ is integer-valued at $\mathcal H^s$-almost every point of $K$. One of its countably many integer level sets therefore has positive $\mathcal H^s$ measure. Choose a compact positive-measure subset $K_Q$ of that level set. Repeating the preceding argument with $K_Q$, choose a fixed point $p\in K_Q$ and radii $L_i\downarrow0$ on which its normalized $s$-mass is bounded below. The resulting tangent cone is a $Q$-plane. Translate $p$ to zero, denote that plane by $\Pi$, and set $$\sigma_i=L_i^{-s}(\eta_{p,L_i})_\#
       \big(\mathcal H^s\!\llcorner
          (K_Q\cap\overline{\mathbf B}_{L_i}(p))\big).$$ Compactness of $K_Q$ ensures that the support consists entirely of points from that integer-density level set. Dilation preserves both density and singularity. The asserted convergence and bounds follow. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:normal-inputs"></a>
### Normal cylinders and signed excess

The next construction compares the varifold with a smooth reference disk, not necessarily a plane. We fix the geometric conventions and state the two measure estimates from (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)] used throughout the proof. The local minimal-fitting statements will be introduced when the reference disk is constructed in Section <a href="#sec:fitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fitting">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Geometry and units

Let $S$ be a small-slope $C^J$ graph over an $m$-plane, with a fixed normal tube and uniform bounds through a sufficiently large finite order $J$. In that tube write $$X=y+z,\qquad y=\pi(X)\in S,\qquad z\in N_yS.$$ The horizontal and vertical subspaces are $T_yS$ and $N_yS$. Let $P_T$ denote orthogonal projection onto the approximate tangent plane of the varifold at $X$. For $v\in N_yS$, the shape operator $\mathcal S_v$ is defined by $$\langle\mathcal S_v a,b\rangle
   =\langle\mathrm{II}_S(a,b),v\rangle,
 \qquad a,b\in T_yS.$$ Set <a id="eq:normal-tensors"></a>$$\label{eq:normal-tensors}
 B=I-(P_T)_{hh},\qquad e=\operatorname{tr}B=\operatorname{tr}(P_T)_{vv},
 \qquad J_z=I-\mathcal S_z,\qquad K_z=J_z^{-1}.$$ The tube is chosen so that $J_z$ and $K_z$ are uniformly bounded. The tensor $B$ is positive semidefinite, $0\le e\le m$, and <a id="eq:projection-blocks"></a>$$\label{eq:projection-blocks}
 (P_T)_{hv}(P_T)_{vh}=B-B^2,
 \qquad |(P_T)_{vh}a|^2\le B[a,a],
 \qquad |(P_T)_{vh}|\le C\sqrt e.$$ These follow by taking blocks in $P_T^2=P_T$.

We restrict to an interior cylinder whose support is separated from its artificial vertical boundary above every compact subset of the base. Every field has compact base support and is multiplied by a vertical cutoff equal to one near the relevant support. Thus the restriction has no vertical boundary term in its first variation. All enlargements below stay inside this cylinder.

For the positive integer $Q$ being considered, define measures on $S$ by <a id="eq:normal-measures"></a>$$\label{eq:normal-measures}
 M=\pi_\#\mu-Q\operatorname{vol}_S,\qquad D=\pi_\#(e\mu).$$ Here $M$ is signed and $D$ is nonnegative. In a specified physical system of Euclidean units, let $z_{\mathrm{phys}}$ denote the unscaled normal displacement and put <a id="eq:physical-error"></a>$$\label{eq:physical-error}
 \Lambda=\pi_\#(\lambda\mu),\qquad
 \lambda=|z_{\mathrm{phys}}|^2+|\mathcal H_S|^2
                       +|\nabla^\perp\mathcal H_S|^2,
 \qquad \mathcal H_S=\operatorname{tr}\mathrm{II}_S.$$ The curvature and its derivative in this formula are evaluated in those physical units. On scaling a base chart of physical radius $l$ to unit radius, all measures, including reference volume, are pushed forward and divided by $l^m$. The factor $\lambda$ is still evaluated physically; it is not replaced by the squared rescaled height or the rescaled curvature. For a flat reference plane the estimates of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)] use $\Lambda=0$. Charging an additional nonnegative height term in an upper bound does not weaken their applicability.

<a id="eq:physical-gamma"></a>
Normal frames are transported radially from the chart center, and base coordinates are normalized to have Euclidean metric at that center. For an interior subchart using the inherited frames, the small geometric parameter remains the original $$\label{eq:physical-gamma}
 \gamma=l+\sup|z_{\mathrm{phys}}|.$$ This convention matters when the signed-excess theorem is used on much smaller cells: changing the coordinates alone does not improve a physical curvature error.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The two imported measure estimates

The first statement identifies an integer using a smooth projected mass average, then controls the total variation of $M$. The projected count $N(y)$ counts normal-fiber entries with their integer multiplicities at almost every base point; exceptional projection fibers are immaterial for the statement.

<a id="stmt-lem-scalar-input"></a>
<div id="lem:scalar-input" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:scalar-distribution-before-count"></a>
<a id="eq:scalar-input"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Scalar mass and propagation of the integer). *Fix dimensions, a bound for $Q$, bounded chart geometry, a mass bound, and an interior enlargement factor $c>1$. Suppose $$s^{-m}\pi_\#\mu(B_{cs})\le C_0,
 \qquad s^{-m}(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs})\le\epsilon$$ with $\epsilon$ sufficiently small. Put $b=(1+c)/2$. On the intermediate ball $B_{bs}$, the count selects an integer $q\ge0$ with $\operatorname{vol}_S\{y\in B_{bs}:N(y)\ne q\}\le C\epsilon s^m$. There is a fixed finite test order $J_{\mathrm{sc}}$ such that, for every smooth scalar test $\eta$ supported in $B_{bs}$, $$\label{eq:scalar-distribution-before-count}
 \begin{aligned}
 \left|\int\eta\,\mathrm d(\pi_\#\mu-q\operatorname{vol}_S)\right|
 &\le C\epsilon s^m\|\eta\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s},\\
 \|\eta\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s}
 &=\max_{0\le j\le J_{\mathrm{sc}}}s^j\|D^j\eta\|_\infty.
 \end{aligned}$$ The derivatives are in base coordinates; this test order is included among the finite orders fixed for the argument. For every nonnegative smooth test $\chi$ supported in $B_{bs}$, with reference integral one and $s^m\|\chi\|_{C^{J_{\mathrm{sc}}}_s}\le C_1$ for a fixed constant $C_1$, one also has <a id="eq:scalar-average-before-count"></a>$$\label{eq:scalar-average-before-count}
 \left|\int\chi\,\mathrm d(\pi_\#\mu)-q\right|\le C\epsilon.$$ Here the constant may depend on $C_1$; the factor $s^m$ accounts for the size of a test whose integral, rather than its supremum, is one. In particular, if such an average is within $1/4$ of $Q$, then $q=Q$, and $$\label{eq:scalar-input}
 |M|(B_s)+\operatorname{vol}_S\{y\in B_s:N(y)\ne Q\}
                     \le C(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs}).$$ The same integer propagates along comparable working balls $B_s$ satisfying these bounds, provided their overlaps have fixed positive relative volume. The smallness threshold depends on that overlap fraction.*

</div>

This is (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 2.5 and its proof) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)], including its distributional, smooth-average, and overlap conclusions. The distributional estimate first compares the projected measure with a scalar constant; the integer-count argument places that constant within $C\epsilon$ of $q$. It therefore gives Equation <a href="#eq:scalar-distribution-before-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-distribution-before-count">[eq:scalar-distribution-before-count]</a> before any identification with $Q$. The enlargement $c$ can be any fixed number greater than one, by a finite interior covering. In particular we use $c=2$ when the room has already been fixed. The integer must be identified before applying Equation <a href="#eq:scalar-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-input">[eq:scalar-input]</a>; a bound for the tilt alone does not specify which integer to subtract.

For a positive integer $j$, write $\exp_j$ and $\log_j$ for $j$-fold composition. For sufficiently large $T$, let <a id="eq:signed-rate"></a>$$\label{eq:signed-rate}
 F_Q(T)=\exp_{2Q}\bigl(\sqrt{\log_{2Q}T}\bigr).$$ We will use only the properties $$(\log T)^a=o(F_Q(T))\quad\text{for every fixed }a>0,
 \qquad F_Q(T)=T^{o(1)}.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-signed-input"></a>
<div id="thm:signed-input" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Signed excess). *Fix an integer $Q\ge1$ and $\kappa>0$. In the normal-cylinder setting above, scale a physical base radius $l$ to one and suppose $B_4$ is available. Set $\tau=|M|+D+\Lambda$. If <a id="eq:signed-hypotheses"></a>$$\label{eq:signed-hypotheses}
 \tau(B_4)\le d=e^{-T},\qquad
 l+\sup|z_{\mathrm{phys}}|\le d^\kappa,$$ where the supremum is over the restricted support above the available chart, then, for $T$ sufficiently large, <a id="eq:signed-input"></a>$$\label{eq:signed-input}
 \int w\,\mathrm d(2M-D-C_Q\Lambda)
       \le d\exp[-F_Q(T)]$$ whenever $w$ is supported in $B_1$, $0\le w\le1$, and $\operatorname{Lip}w\le1$. The finite derivative order $J$, threshold, and constants depend only on the stated fixed data. For a subchart in inherited frames, the second condition is imposed on $\gamma$ in Equation <a href="#eq:physical-gamma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-gamma">[eq:physical-gamma]</a>. For a flat reference, take $\Lambda=0$ and omit the second condition.*

</div>

This is (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)]. Multiplying the test by a fixed constant allows any fixed Lipschitz bound, with that constant on the right-hand side. The theorem does not assume that the multiplicity is bounded by $Q$: $Q$ is the integer subtracted in $M$.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Fixed parameters

<a id="eq:parameter-order"></a>
All dimensional constants and interior enlargement factors are fixed before small scales are sent to zero. Fix an integer kernel exponent $p\ge4$, put $J_0=m+p+4$, and choose $$\label{eq:parameter-order}
 0<\beta_1<\tfrac14,\qquad
 0<\zeta<\min\{1,1/m\},\qquad \kappa>0,\qquad
 \kappa(2J_0+4)<2\beta_1,\qquad 3\kappa<\zeta.$$ We then fix derivative orders large enough for Theorem <a href="#thm:signed-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signed-input">4</a> and the compactness arguments below. Finally we choose the fitting order and its small height coefficient in Section <a href="#sec:fitting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fitting">4</a>. This order is noncircular: $J_0$, $\beta_1$, $\zeta$, and $\kappa$ do not depend on the fitting order. The two inequalities in Equation <a href="#eq:parameter-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameter-order">[eq:parameter-order]</a> will respectively permit the signed comparison in the frequency estimate and in the height-normalized limit.

<a id="sec:fitting"></a>
### Fitting intervals and frequency control

Fix the singular origin, integer $Q$, plane $\Pi$, and target scales $L_i\downarrow0$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:flat-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:flat-scales">2</a>. The scales $L_i$ retain the measure of density-$Q$ centers, but were not chosen by any height or frequency criterion. We shall cover them by intervals on which a fitted center has uniform height doubling. The construction of the local fits comes from (OpenAI 2026, sec. 7) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)]; the selection and comparison of successive fitting intervals are needed here because the origin need not satisfy a typical-point multiplicity condition.

At each start, local minimal graphs fit the support until their height test first fails. We glue these graphs to one reference disk. The failure scales determine the smallest radius on which we estimate its height and frequency. There are then two possibilities. If this radius is zero, the frequency estimates apply inward indefinitely. Otherwise we restart at a smaller scale where the varifold is again close to a plane. On late intervals carrying the targets, the preceding failure bounds the frequency at the outer end, and the new failure bounds it at the inner end. These two endpoint bounds are what make height doubling uniform. The last subsection proves that the resulting intervals contain every sufficiently late $L_i$; no coverage of arbitrary small scales is required.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Local fits and their gluing

A *good start* is a sufficiently small dyadic scale $R$ for which $V_R$ is within a fixed small weak-varifold distance of a multiplicity-$Q$ plane through the origin in $\mathbf B_{2000}$. Use a metric on varifolds with bounded mass, and choose a plane whose distance realizes, or approximates within a fixed factor, the best distance. In particular, if this distance tends to zero, the corresponding varifolds converge to their chosen planes after taking a subsequence. Throughout a start we work in units of $R$ and take its chosen plane as horizontal; $\mu$ denotes the weight of $V_R$ until target units are introduced explicitly.

The goodness threshold will be decreased a finite number of times below. Weak convergence to a plane, support convergence, and monotonicity then provide a cylinder with base containing $B_{300}$, fixed vertical room, and a support-free gap at its vertical boundary. From now on $\mu$ is restricted to this working cylinder, as in Section <a href="#sec:normal-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:normal-inputs">3</a>; every support condition in the fitting construction refers to this restriction. On its interior we have the upper mass bound $\mu(\mathbf B_u(X))\le C u^m$ at all radii used below, arbitrarily small support height and weak mass error, and small tilt. The scalar estimate of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[AE](#ref-AE)] also gives small projected mass defect on smaller cylinders. These statements concern all support points in the cylinder, not a selected collection of sheets. Since the density at the origin is $Q$, we require the start to be small enough that all needed smaller and fixed-factor larger density ratios at the origin are uniformly as close to $Q$ as prescribed.

<a id="eq:fitting-order"></a>
Choose a finite center derivative order $J>m/2+6$, larger than the orders needed for the signed estimate and subsequent compactness arguments. Apply the fitting lemma with order at least $J+1$; denote its exponent $k_0$ by $k$. In particular, $$\label{eq:fitting-order}
 k>4+m/2.$$ Next choose its height parameter $m_0>0$ sufficiently small. We recall precisely the fitting information to be used.

<a id="stmt-lem-local-fits"></a>
<div id="lem:local-fits" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Local fitting inputs). *For every $x\in B_5$, there are smooth minimal graphs $S_{x,\ell}=\operatorname{graph}f_{x,\ell}$ over $B_{80\ell}(x)$, at descending dyadic scales $\ell=2^{-j}$ starting at $j=0$, through the first scale at which $$\sup\{|X_v-f_{x,\ell}(X_h)|:
          X\in\operatorname{spt}\mu,\ X_h\in B_{64\ell}(x)\}
                                  \le m_0\ell^k$$ fails. Every constructed fit, including the first failed one, has support height at most $Cm_0\ell^k$ on the fixed interior enlargements of this test ball.
